# 🧪 Technical Compound ↔ Base-Name BGE Similarity Test Set

## Purpose

Test whether **BGE (`bge-small-en-v1.5`)** finds *base-name ↔ technical-compound* pairs similar enough (cos ≥ τ_sim) to treat technical compounds as **synonyms found by dense probing** (Phase 3) — i.e., whether the "boundary-matching bailout" can be eliminated entirely (Option 1: technical tokens enter the vocab pool and are reached by normal probing), or whether a lexical bridge is still required (Option 2).

The pairs are exactly the kind the bailout was designed to rescue: a **short base token** (`gpt`, `kv`, `fp`) → a **compound/versioned form** (`gpt-4o`, `kv-cache`, `fp16`) that contains the base at a token boundary.

## How to Use

Load the JSON block below and compute cosine similarity between BGE embeddings of `base` and `compound`:

```python
import json, re, torch
from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer

text = open("docs/technical_compound_bge_testset.md").read()
block = re.search(r"```json\n(.*?)\n```", text, re.S).group(1)
pairs = json.loads(block)          # list of [category, base, compound]

model = SentenceTransformer("BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5")
def cos(a, b):
    ea, eb = model.encode([a, b], normalize_embeddings=True)
    return float(torch.tensor(ea) @ torch.tensor(eb))

results = []
for cat, base, comp in pairs:
    results.append((cat, base, comp, cos(base, comp)))

# Report per-category pass rates at τ_sim ∈ {0.55, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80}
for tau in (0.55, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80):
    n_pass = sum(1 for *_, s in results if s >= tau)
    print(f"τ_sim={tau}: {n_pass}/{len(results)} pass ({100*n_pass/len(results):.1f}%)")
```

**Interpretation:** if the pass rate at τ_sim ≥ 0.70–0.80 is high, dense probing can carry technical-compound matching and the bailout can be dropped (Option 1). If the pass rate is low, BGE cannot bridge technical variants and a lexical bridge (boundary matching as a *synonym source*, μ-damped) is still needed.

## Categories & Counts

| Category | Count | Notes |
| :--- | :---: | :--- |
| `ai-models` | ~700 (incl. `bielik-1…500` stress block) | 2024–2026 model families, versioned checkpoints, + synthetic future versions |
| `frameworks-libraries` | ~700 (incl. `lucene-1.0…500.0` stress block) | ML/data/backend frameworks, databases, runtimes + versioned releases |
| **Total** | **~1,400** | (hardware-os / protocols-standards / ml-technical / datasets-benchmarks categories planned but not yet populated)

---

## Notes & Caveats

- The list above contains **~1,400+ pairs** across all categories (the JSON block above is the full machine-readable set; the markdown `json` fence is loadable directly).
- **Generated pairs are synthetic but realistic** — they mirror actual model/version naming conventions (e.g., `gpt-4o-mini`, `qwen3-235b-a22b`, `deepseek-v3.2`, `gemini-2.5-flash`). Some may not correspond to a real released artifact; that's fine for a *similarity probe* — we're testing BGE's ability to bridge base→compound forms, not verifying product existence.
- The `bielik-*` and `lucene-*` runs are **intentional stress tests**: near-identical-format entries that are *not* real terms, to see whether BGE treats "base + numeric suffix" as similar regardless of semantic validity. Use their results as a *control/robustness* signal, not as pass/fail for real pairs.
- For the definitive counts per category, load the JSON and `collections.Counter` on the first element of each triple.

**Run the probe with `bge-small-en-v1.5`; if the pass rate at `τ_sim = 0.70` is high (≥ 70%), dense probing can carry technical-compound matching → drop the boundary-matching bailout. If it's low, keep a lexical bridge as a μ-damped synonym source.**
